MANUAL COMPLETION — 28 Oct 2026
==============================================================================

The collector run reported 1 failure. It is resolved and the file is
in this tree, so the "Failures: 1" the run printed describes the run
and not the result. The fix is recorded in the per-file entry below
as well, on its "manual" and "verified" lines.

  libyuv 0.0.1928-r0  (alpine, image fe)
      A sha512 mismatch, and a correct refusal: the bytes the run
      received are not the bytes recorded in sha512sums=. What makes
      this one different from an ordinary mismatch is that NO source
      can supply those bytes any more, including Alpine's own.

      source= points at chromium.googlesource.com/+archive/<commit>,
      which generates its tarball per request. Two downloads seconds
      apart returned 579,903 and 579,824 bytes with different
      hashes. The distfiles mirrors differ again: v3.24 is 579,770
      bytes and edge is 579,825. The archive is not reproducible and
      the sha512sums= line records one generation of it that has not
      been reproducible since.

      Alpine knows this, and the APKBUILD in this tree says so. It
      carries a second checksum over the UNPACKED tree, and its
      unpack() verifies that instead of the archive:

          _libyuvchecksum=b92d034854b4b1f5b67ec10aec9ff242c5a46597…
          unpack() {
              tar xzf "$srcdir"/libyuv-0.0.1928.tar.gz -C "$builddir"
              cd "$builddir"
              find . -type f | sort | xargs cat | sha512sum \
                  | grep -q "$_libyuvchecksum"
          }

      That is the check applied here, and it is the package's own.
      The archive in this tree was taken from

        https://distfiles.alpinelinux.org/distfiles/v3.24/libyuv-0.0.1928.tar.gz

      the branch of the Alpine release the "fe" image runs (3.24.2).
      Unpacked it is 190 files and 5,214,994 bytes, and

          find . -type f | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs cat | sha512sum

      over them is b92d034854b4b1f5b67ec10aec9ff242c5a46597fc1f02e9
      2feb9defa73fe863abd6c45140e54cb4b76a5831830bcaf695eff44c6362f
      aedd47817051683f80d — the APKBUILD's value exactly. LC_ALL=C
      matters: the sort order is part of what is hashed, and a
      locale-sensitive sort produces a different digest from the
      same files.

      The other two generations above unpack to the same 190 files
      and the same digest, so this is a property of the archive
      format and not of any one mirror.

      This is a weaker claim than the rest of this tree carries. An
      ordinary entry here is verified against the publisher's own
      record of the archive bytes; this one is verified against the
      publisher's own record of the archive CONTENTS. It is the
      strongest check that exists for this package, and it is the
      one Alpine itself builds on, but it is not the same check and
      is not recorded as though it were.

  The INCOMPLETE marker the run wrote was removed once this was
  resolved, which is what the collector itself does when a run ends
  with no failures.

NOT COLLECTED HERE, and why. This tree is complete for what it
claims and no more; read this before citing it as discharging any
obligation.

  158 permissive Debian source packages.
  They owe attribution, not source, and every Debian
  package installs /usr/share/doc/<pkg>/copyright inside
  the delivered image — so their attribution travels with
  the product and their source discharges nothing.

Debian source is the .dsc / .orig.tar / .debian.tar set published for
the exact installed version. Alpine source is the APKBUILD, every file
beside it in the aports directory, and the upstream archive that
APKBUILD patches.

The upstream archive is here for EVERY Alpine package, not only the
copyleft ones. For the copyleft packages it is the corresponding
source and is owed as such. For the permissive ones it is where
their licence text lives: Alpine installs no licence text in the
image for all but 6 of them, so these archives are how the
attribution those packages do owe reaches the recipient. Section
5.9 of THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.txt states that route; this is it.

The aports ref each APKBUILD was taken from is recorded per package
below. It is the commit apk itself recorded for the installed package
(the c: field of /lib/apk/db/installed), which is the exact tree the
package was built from; where apk recorded no commit, the tag of the
Alpine release the image runs was used, and failing that the stable
branch. Never master. Whichever ref answered, the pkgver in the
APKBUILD was compared against the installed version before the file was
kept, so the recipe corresponds to the binary that shipped.

VERIFICATION. Two different hashes appear per file and they do
different jobs:

  "sha256" is of the file as delivered here. It lets the recipient
  confirm that what is on this machine is what this manifest
  describes. It says nothing about where the bytes came from.

  "verified" names the algorithm used to check the file against a
  checksum published by whoever published the archive -- the hash
  snapshot.debian.org's index returns, the Checksums-Sha256 stanza of
  a .dsc, or the sha512sums= block of the APKBUILD. That check ran
  during collection and a mismatch aborted the package rather than
  being recorded, so no file below carries bytes that contradict its
  publisher. This is what makes the tree corresponding source and not
  merely plausible source: upstream projects do re-roll release
  tarballs under the same filename.

  "verified : no checksum was published for this file" means exactly
  that. The file is delivered and its delivered hash is recorded, but
  no upstream value existed to check it against. It is stated per file
  rather than left as an absence.

  A "mirror" line means the URL the APKBUILD names did not answer and
  the archive was taken from somewhere else: Alpine's own distfiles
  mirror, or alpinelinux's GitHub mirror of a gitlab.alpinelinux.org
  repository. Both hold the same archives Alpine builds from, and the
  substitution is only sound because of the "verified" line beside it
  -- the bytes were checked against the APKBUILD's own sha512sums, so a
  mirror could not supply a different archive than the one Alpine used.
  Where the URL is shown, it is shown in full so the substitution can
  be checked rather than taken on trust.

  Files taken from the aports tree itself -- the APKBUILD and the
  patches and scripts beside it -- carry no "verified" line, and that
  is not an omission. There is no separate checksum to check them
  against, because the ref they were fetched at IS the guarantee: a
  git commit names its own content, so a file retrieved at the commit
  apk recorded cannot be a different file than the one Alpine built
  from. The ref is on the "ref" line of each Alpine entry.
